## Consumer, media & industrial

- `9` **OpenAI Publishes First Jalapeño Benchmarks Against Nvidia Blackwell**
  OpenAI published first SemiAnalysis InferenceX numbers for Broadcom-designed Jalapeño: a 700W A0 part hit up to 1.9x throughput per kW versus a 1,400W Nvidia Blackwell flagship and 1.7–3.6x lower end-to-end latency across GPT-OSS 120B, DeepSeek R1 and Kimi K2.5 1T (700 tok/s on single-user DeepSeek R1). SemiAnalysis, which witnessed but did not independently run the suite, calls the Blackwell comparison incomplete because Jalapeño uses HBM4 (15.4 TB/s) while the tested Nvidia systems do not — like-for-like is Rubin — and the jobs were short-context/single-turn on OpenAI-supplied figures; B0 is already in fab. Deployment is slated inside OpenAI’s own fleet before end-2026 (volume 2027), with Gen 2 in development and continued Nvidia purchases. This is an inflection datapoint for AVGO custom silicon and the InferenceX/openai-xpu questions: it supports a Broadcom XPU cost-curve/financing narrative (including versus Nvidia’s $105B underwrite) but does not yet prove watt share shift. Confirm or refute with an independent InferenceX rerun versus Rubin/HBM4 on multi-turn agent traffic, and whether 2027 Jalapeño watts actually displace GB300/Rubin rather than sit alongside them.
  Sources: [forbes.com](https://forbes.com/sites/jonmarkman/2026/08/27/openai-publishes-first-jalapeo-benchmarks-against-nvidia-blackwell) · [global.techapple.com](https://global.techapple.com/2026/08/openai-publishes-first-benchmarks-for-its-in-house-jalapeno-inference-chip)
- `8` **OpenAI's Jalapeño Chip Outperforms NVIDIA's Blackwell in Efficiency | KuCoin**
  SemiAnalysis, after a lab visit and InferenceX runs with OpenAI engineers, reports that Broadcom-designed Jalapeño (16-month cycle from mid-2024 tape-out) beats Nvidia Blackwell on performance-per-watt in nearly every tested scenario and outperformed Nvidia, AMD and Google chips on open-source models; it uses HBM4 (profile closer to Rubin than GB300), is general-purpose rather than OpenAI-model-specific, and is slated to enter OpenAI infrastructure by year-end while Vera Rubin is already shipping. CUDA absence and GPU flexibility remain Nvidia’s cited offsets; Omdia sees ASIC volumes overtaking GPUs by 2028 even if GPU revenue stays higher longer. This is an inflection-point datapoint for openai-xpu and inferencex and for medium-weight AVGO (custom-silicon partner, demand/share) versus NVDA (inference margins, OpenAI historically a top customer, hyperscalers ~half of AI infra spend): transmission is custom-ASIC share shift and power-constrained TCO, not near-term GPU unit collapse. Confirm or refute with year-end volume, a public InferenceX/MLPerf print versus Rubin, and whether hyperscaler FY27 capex still funds both stacks.

## Custom silicon & compute

- `10` **NVIDIA Moves Memory Controller Into HBM Stack - Converge Digest**
  Converge Digest supplies the mechanism and first customer the NVIDIA blog omitted: NVHBM moves NVIDIA’s memory controller off the XPU compute die into the HBM base die, claiming up to 30% more bandwidth and 15% lower HBM power vs HBM4E, and up to 25% more XPU compute-die area (vs “up to 30%” in NVIDIA’s blog — sources disagree on the area save). NVIDIA will standardize NVHBM across multiple memory suppliers using the same technology as future NVIDIA GPUs; Amazon Annapurna is the first named collaborator, with AWS NVLink Fusion support starting on Trainium4 so Trainium and NVIDIA GPUs share MGX racks (extending the Dec-2025 Fusion commitment). Touches nvhbm-custom, mrvl-trainium, and custom-silicon vs NVDA: AWS is taking NVIDIA’s controller IP and scale-up fabric while keeping Annapurna silicon — a NVDA share-retention path around XPUs, not full displacement. Confirm if SK hynix/Samsung/Micron disclose NVHBM base-die qualification on Trainium4 or Rubin; refute if Trainium4 ships JEDEC HBM4 with an on-die controller or uses UALink/Ethernet for scale-up.

- `10` **NVIDIA Data Center Revenue Hits $89B as Vera Rubin Moves Into Production - Converge Digest**
  NVIDIA FQ2 FY27 revenue was $96.2B (+18% QoQ, +106% YoY) with Data Center $89.0B (+18% QoQ, +117% YoY, >92% of sales), GAAP operating income $63.7B (+124% YoY), and FQ3 guided to $108B ±2% with no China DC compute and GM ~74% ±50 bps (Q2 75.0%). Vera Rubin is in full production with racks already running at CoreWeave, Google Cloud, Azure, OCI and Nebius; content per GW is cited at ~$18B Hopper / ~$25B Grace Blackwell / ~$40B Vera Rubin, NeoClouds are expected to exit 2026 at ~8 GW vs ~3 GW end-2025, and top-five hyperscalers are pegged at ~$800B capex in 2026 and $1.3T in 2027. FY28 customer forecasts could support ~100% growth but NVIDIA expects ~70% on supply; AWS is adding 2 million GPUs through Q2 FY29 plus Vera CPUs, Spectrum-6 is shipping pluggable and CPO, and financing partners (Apollo, BlackRock, Blackstone, Brookfield, Goldman, KKR) target >$500B of third-party AI-infra capital. This is an inflection print for the book: NBIS is a named live Rubin-rack customer into the November Q3 energised-MW vs contracted-GW test, CRWV likewise, the $40B/GW Rubin attach plus CPO lifts NVDA/AVGO/MRVL and 000660 HBM4 intensity, and $1.3T 2027 hyperscaler spend is the first hard FY27 demand figure versus the July reset—confirm if late-October hyperscaler capex prints match, refute if Q3 Rubin mix or the China-zero assumption slips.

- `10` **AWS Links Trainium Strategy with NVIDIA NVLink, NVHBM and Vera - Converge Digest**
  AWS will add 2 million NVIDIA GPUs (Blackwell Ultra, Rubin, Rubin Ultra) across 2027–2028 after already committing >1 million starting 2026, with AWS stating demand from frontier labs, enterprises and governments has already exceeded that earlier plan; a 100k-GPU U.S. government AI-factory tranche at IL6+ is included. The co-engineering layer is the new fact: Annapurna will extend NVLink Fusion onto NVIDIA’s custom NVHBM so next-gen Trainium can tap NVIDIA memory and scale-up fabric while Nitro/EFA remain the scale-out/security stack, plus Vera CPU instances, Spectrum clustering, and Bedrock/SageMaker Nemotron. For NVDA (medium book) this is a named 2027–28 Rubin-ramp volume lock and a demand signal that hyperscaler GPU offtake is still expanding, not substituting; for nvhbm-custom and 000660 it implies NVIDIA is pulling custom HBM base-die into AWS’s Trainium path, which would confirm if TSMC/CoWoS and HBM4 cube allocations for NVHBM show up in 2027 supply. For mrvl-fabric/mrvl-trainium it is an architecture win for NVLink Fusion on Trainium, not a Marvell volume print—confirm only if Marvell or Alchip is later named on the Trainium/NVHBM package. Inflection vs incremental: the 2M add on top of 1M+ is a step-change AWS capacity number, not a refresh of the 2026 guide.
  Sources: [convergedigest.com](https://convergedigest.com/aws-nvidia-2-million-gpus-ai-infrastructure)

  Broadcom and OpenAI taped out Jalapeño, a custom LLM-inference ASIC, in nine months design-to-tape-out, with initial deployment targeted by end-2026; Hock Tan frames it as the start of a multi-generation platform. AVGO AI semiconductor revenue is the hard print: $8.4B (+106% YoY) in FQ1’26, $10.8B (+143%) in FQ2, with FQ3 AI growth guided >200% YoY (FQ4’25 AI +74% on $18.01B total). Separate from OpenAI, the piece cites a $10B then $11B Anthropic order for TPU Ironwood racks by late 2026, and Mizuho at ~$120B AVGO AI revenue by 2027. For AVGO (medium) and openai-xpu/avgo-demand this is the Jalapeño + Anthropic-TPU gigawatt stack the book is watching versus NVIDIA; it is an inflection if FQ3 actually prints >200% AI growth and Jalapeño wafers start before YE26. Confirm/refute: OpenAI 10GW custom vs NVIDIA split, and whether MediaTek TPU/ASIC (avgo-bear) or VMware/CISPE noise offsets the semiconductor beat.

- `9` **🌐 AMD reveals MI400 architecture and MI455X chip at Hot Chips 2026**
  At Hot Chips 2026 AMD specified Helios as a 72-GPU rack at 2.9 exaflops, 31 TB HBM4 and 1.7 PB/s HBM bandwidth, with 260 TB/s scale-up and 43 TB/s scale-out; each tray pairs four modular Instinct MI455X with one EPYC 9006 SP7 via Infinity Fabric. MI455X is eight N2 complex dies plus N3P I/O, 256 WGPs, 192 MB global L2, 12 HBM4 stacks (432 GB at 23.3 TB/s; 1.5× capacity and ~2.9× HBM bandwidth vs MI355X’s 288 GB HBM3E), PCIe Gen 6 and 72-lane UALoE at 3.6 TB/s, with peak 40.26 PF MXFP4 (4× MI355X), 20.13 PF MXFP6/8 and 315 TF FP16/FP32 (up to 2×). ROCm is pitched with “AI Skills” for Claude, Codex, Cursor and Gemini plus Hyperloom. This is the amd-parity architecture drop versus NVDA (and an N2/HBM4 demand signal for TSM and HBM names); UALoE is AMD’s own scale-up fabric in the text, not UALink. Confirm via Helios shipments and InferenceX/MLPerf versus Rubin/TPU v7; refute if 12-stack HBM4 cubes or ROCm.AI fail to show in production training.

- `9` **Broadcom stock gets fresh AI boost as OpenAI showcases Jalapeno chip performance**
  On 26 Aug 2026 OpenAI published Jalapeño InferenceX results versus NVIDIA Blackwell systems: higher single-user token throughput and AI work per kilowatt, and 1.7–3.6× lower end-to-end latency across three models; limited volumes by YE26, meaningful rollout in 2027, with follow-on generations already in development. AVGO last close cited ~$360.50 (intraday $356.60–$363.80 on 25 Aug). For AVGO this converts Jalapeño from a design-win headline into a public efficiency claim against GB200-class inference, i.e. share of OpenAI serving watts rather than a training displacement. For inferencex/NVDA it is the Q3-benchmark datapoint the book flagged: confirm if SemiAnalysis/InferenceX is third-party and includes Rubin/HBM4 systems, refute if fleet TCO, multi-turn agent traces, or reliability are not shown. - `9` **OpenAI Jalapeño Results: What the Chip Means for NVIDIA | LLM Rumors**
  LLM Rumors/OpenAI-reported InferenceX: Jalapeño did 1.5–1.9× higher peak work per package-TDP watt and 1.7–3.6× lower E2E latency than selected GB200/GB300 systems on three public models; AI-assisted design cut tape-out to nine months, ported three unplanned model families in two months, and produced kernels 1.5–1.8× faster than prior human code. Explicitly not a training/CUDA replacement: inference-only, initial deploy by YE26 while OpenAI keeps buying NVIDIA for training and inference, Gen 2 already deep, plus separate forward-looking 10GW arrangements with both Broadcom custom accelerators and NVIDIA; Celestica on board/rack. For AVGO/openai-xpu this supports multi-sourcing at GW scale, not a supplier swap; for NVDA it is an inference-economics warning (tokens per watt, negotiation leverage) that still leaves training and most 2026–27 fleet on NVIDIA. Caveat in-source: results do not establish fleet cost, reliability, or long multi-turn agent traffic—those are the falsifiers before treating Jalapeño as a 2027 NVIDIA inference share-shift.
  Sources: [llmrumors.com](https://llmrumors.com/news/openai-jalapeno-nvidia-inference-chip)
- `9` **In just 9 months, has OpenAI's in-house ASIC chip already surpassed Nvidia's Blackwell? - Odaily**
  Odaily/Wallstreetcn/SemiAnalysis layer on more aggressive (and internally inconsistent) figures: GPT-OSS 120B at 1,459 tok/s vs GB200 535 (2.7×) and 1.65s E2E vs GB300 6s (3.6×); 53M tok/s per MW vs ~10M for GB200 NVL72; claimed TCO ~$1.56/chip-hour vs Vera Rubin $3.61; HBM4 at 15.4 TB/s and 22 vs 11.1 HBM BW per watt; design-to-tape-out cited as ~16 months with CoWoS tape-out Nov 2025 (~9 months ago). B0 in fab for +25% perf/W; 10GW Broadcom custom-accelerator pact; mass production ramp 2027, 100MW as next scale milestone. These watt and TCO numbers are stronger than items 4–5 and should not be blended: item 5’s 1.5–1.9× work/W vs GB200/300 is the conservative OpenAI-reported band; the 5× tokens/MW and $1.56 vs $3.61 Rubin TCO are SemiAnalysis/secondary and the piece itself warns the test is not a frontier/AgentX multi-turn model and a fair compare is Vera Rubin (HBM4), not Blackwell. For AVGO this still flags Jalapeño as an InferenceX inflection if 100MW lands in 2027; for NVDA/000660/hbm4 it implies Jalapeño is an HBM4 customer, so custom-ASIC HBM demand adds to Rubin rather than replacing it. CUDA-moat-is-dead is opinion, not a measured software-maturity result.
  Sources: [odaily.news](https://odaily.news/en/post/5212700)

- `8` **Intel unveils a 256-core Diamond Rapids Xeon for 2027 - BuySellRam**
  At Hot Chips on 24 August Intel detailed Diamond Rapids (Xeon 7) for 2H 2027: 256 P-cores on 18A-P, double the 128 in today’s Xeon 6980P, but with SMT dropped so thread count stays 256 (same as 6980P); SMT returns on Coral Rapids. The package is 22 dies — 16×16-core chiplets stacked by Foveros Direct on four 320 MB base tiles (1.28 GB LLC vs 504 MB) plus two fabric hubs that now own all memory controllers, PCIe Gen 6/CXL 3.0 (128 lanes) and I/O, with UCIe-S between base and hubs; memory goes to 16 channels at 12,800 MT/s MRDIMM (~1.6 TB/s peak) with no 8-channel SKU, so 16 DIMMs per socket. This is the intel-18a process-and-packaging proof point (18A-P after the June 288-core E-core Xeon 6+ / Clearwater Forest) and an amd-epyc share fight in 2027: core density and bandwidth jump, but threads do not, and the 22-die/16-DIMM design raises yield, socket and DIMM-cost risk versus AMD. Confirmation is 2H27 revenue shipments and 18A-P yields; a slip, a cut to 256-core attach, or EPYC Venice taking two-socket share would refute.

## Datacenter power & cooling

- `8` **Can Oracle Stock Fund a $638B Backlog Without Breaking Margins? | Trefis**
  Oracle’s named bottleneck has shifted from component delays to funding a $638B backlog (from $130B in early 2025) with ~$70B net FY27 capex and ~$40B of debt/equity against FY26 revenue that just crossed $67B; shares are −36% over 12 months. Delivery is the new fact: >1.2 GW in FY26 and FQ1 FY27 alone expected to approach 1 GW — implying, if delivered, a run-rate far above a parts-rationed business — with most FY26 Q4 AI infra prepaid or BYOH and $20–25B customer prepay/timing explaining why gross capex exceeds the $70B net outlay. FY27 GM is guided down near-term on ramp/mix, OCI is framed at 30–40% once sites fill, and revenue is guided +34% CC. For ORCL this is a demand-not-parts underwrite: booked GW is being built, but FCF and OCI margin in FY27 reports are the falsifier. Confirm FQ1 actually delivers near 1 GW without a further GM miss, and whether prepay stays in the $20–25B range as the funding offset.

- `8` **800 VDC and the Race to Power the AI Factory - Panel Builder US**
  Nvidia is designing an 800 VDC rack backbone for 1 MW-class AI racks (≈1,250 A at 800 V vs >18,500 A at 54 V), while Siemens and Reinhausen are working on solid-state transformers that convert up to 36 kV grid AC directly to 800 VDC to drop conversion stages. The article splits the power path: SiC on AC-to-800 V bus conversion, GaN inside the server for high-frequency, high-current VRMs, with ISOP stacks (e.g. eight 100 V-to-12 V cells) avoiding a single 800 V FET. This is a system-architecture demand signal for the 800vdc, vrt-cooling/vrt-orders and vicr-vpd watches (rack power density, liquid cooling, vertical power at the GPU), not an order print. Confirm with an Nvidia 800 VDC reference design or Vertiv/Vicor design-win language; refute if hyperscalers stay on 48/54 V past the 1 MW-rack timeline.

That ~100% demand vs ~70% supply gap is an allocation/pricing-power signal for NVDA (Medium) and a multi-year floor for memory/WFE (000660, MU, AMAT/LRCX/KLA); hyperscaler late-October CY26 capex vs the July reset would confirm the doubling is funded, while a miss on the $108B Q3 print or earlier easing of the FY28 ceiling would refute it.

  OpenAI published first Jalapeño InferenceX results, claiming 1.5–1.9× peak performance per watt vs Nvidia Blackwell systems after Broadcom delivered the custom inference ASIC to Altman/Brockman in June 2026; tests used GPT-OSS 120B plus DeepSeek R1 670B and Kimi K2.5 1T, with a localized KV cache aimed at both prefill throughput and decode latency for agentic loads. Results were produced by OpenAI, not an independent lab, and normalized to each accelerator’s published power rating—so the 1.5–1.9× is a vendor figure, not a referee score. This is an inflection-style custom-silicon datapoint for AVGO (Jalapeño/openai-xpu) versus NVDA share, and for inferencex; it also sits next to OpenAI’s Cerebras-powered Ultrafast tier (up to 14× Standard / 750 tok/s on GPT-5.6 Sol), so CBRS remains in the mix as a separate speed path. Confirmation is a third-party InferenceX/MLPerf drop and disclosed Jalapeño GW/wafer volumes; a restated gap nearer 1.0× or delayed Broadcom ramps would refute a near-term Nvidia inference displacement.

## Memory & storage

- `10` **SK Hynix: HBM4E remains with MR-MUF, HBM5 with Hybrid Bonding**
  At Hot Chips 2026, SK Hynix America’s packaging VP Jaesik Lee said hybrid bonding is unlikely to be ready for HBM4E, so larger-scale use is earliest with HBM5 (industry mass-production forecasts ~2029–2030); MR-MUF will be iterated through HBM4E. JEDEC raised HBM4 max package height from ~720 µm to 775 µm (aligned to a typical 300-mm logic wafer so the stack and adjacent logic die share a coldplate); 16-Hi HBM4 DRAM dies are ~50 µm thick with tighter inter-die gaps. Thermal load is up 2.2× across generations while pin rates reach 8 Gbit/s on HBM4; SK Hynix is also working on iHBM cooling. For 000660 (High book) and hbm-process/hybrid-bonding/hbm4, this implies HBM4/HBM4E shipping cubes stay on the established MR-MUF yield/cost curve rather than a hybrid-bonding yield cliff, extending the Namics-linked MUF moat into HBM4E and delaying a bonding-tool mix shift. Confirm via HBM4E customer quals and whether Samsung/Micron ship TC-NCF or hybrid-bonded 16-Hi earlier; refute if SK Hynix qualifies a hybrid-bonded HBM4E SKU before HBM5.

  ZDNet Korea, citing industry, says Samsung and SK Hynix will raise 8-Hi (not 12-Hi) as the share of Nvidia HBM4 shipments in 2H after Nvidia asked to switch the main SKU; 12-Hi for Vera Rubin had been on a Samsung 1Q / SK 2Q mass-supply path. This is the samsung-hbm / nvhbm-custom / hybrid-bonding axis: an active base-die strategy that would compete with SK Hynix (000660) MR-MUF cubes and custom HBM base dies, but only if it ships. Confirm on a named GPU customer or HBM4E qualification date; refute if it remains a talk-track while HBM4 cubes ship on existing 2.5D.
  Sources: [cloudnews.tech](https://cloudnews.tech/samsung-prepares-zhbm-3d-memory-on-the-gpu-to-surpass-hbm-limits)
- `9` **SK Hynix's HBM4 Packaging Bet and Nvidia's Earnings Loom Over a Choppy Week**
  At Hot Chips SK Hynix (000660) confirmed Intel Foundry EMIB for HBM4, claiming up to 50% cost vs rival interposers, with HBM4 at 2048-bit / 2 TB/s and +40% energy efficiency vs HBM3E; it will keep Advanced MR-MUF for HBM4E and slip hybrid bonding to HBM5 because of the 775 μm stack-height cap. That is the live 000660 signal: MR-MUF stays on shipping HBM4/HBM4E cubes, EMIB is a cost/packaging hedge (also intel-18a/advanced packaging), and hybrid bonding is not the 2026–27 bottleneck. Stock was +12% on the week / −7.6% in a month / +158% YTD into NVIDIA earnings. Confirm if NVIDIA Rubin HBM4 allocation and Namics EMC follow this packaging split; refute if a customer forces hybrid bonding or CoWoS interposer anyway and the 50% EMIB saving does not show in gross margin.

- `9` **Memory to account for 68% of cloud giant CapEx by 2027**
  TrendForce now has DRAM+NAND at 47% of major CSP total capex in 2026 and 68% in 2027, inside CSP spend itself forecast +98% YoY in 2026 and +50% in 2027. Server DRAM contracts were +64% in 2H25 with ~270% more projected for 2026; enterprise SSD +35% then +235%; some LTAs from Q2 2026 include price ceilings, while HBM is still seen +70–140% in 2027 even as the table has HBM4 ‘mostly flat’ for 2026F. Server DDR5 QoQ is already decelerating (1Q26 +93–98% → 4Q26F +3–8%) as HBM+RDIMM take 51% of 2026 DRAM bits and 2027 server DRAM+HBM bit supply is only +27%. This is a pricing-power/capacity signal into 000660 and SNDK (LTA floors vs Goldman oversupply) and into NVDA (memory inflation as ASP justification, matching the GM cut in item 7); confirm on hyperscaler Q3 CY26 capex prints vs the July reset, refute if 2026 HBM4 stays flat and 2027 HBM +70–140% is walked back.

- `9` **Hot Chips 2026: Analysis Suggests High Bandwidth Flash Is a High-Capacity Memory, Not an HBM Replacement - TechApple Global**
  OXMIQ at Hot Chips 2026 said HBF cannot replace HBM on most workloads: at cost/power parity a 72-GPU rack goes from 20.7TB / 1,584 TB/s (HBM-only) to 294.9TB but only 922 TB/s (~60% bandwidth) HBF-only, with hybrid at 89.3TB. SanDisk’s grades (G1 256GB/384 GB/s; G2 512GB/1.536 TB/s; G3 512GB/3.072 TB/s) offer 8–16× capacity at similar cost, best for write-once MoE expert weights (93% of Kimi-K3’s 1.56TB) and long-context KV, but need 64KB–1MB DMA, endurance management, and vLLM/NVIDIA/AMD drivers that do not exist yet. For low-weight SNDK this is an hbf-flash positioning print, not a near-term HBM displacement of 000660 — OXMIQ’s line is ‘HBM for the rack, HBF for the box.’ Confirm if NVIDIA/AMD announce HBF attach on Rubin/MI455X; refute if software is still unready into 2027.
  Sources: [global.techapple.com](https://global.techapple.com/2026/08/hot-chips-2026-analysis-suggests-high-bandwidth-flash-is-a-high-capacity-memory-not-an-hbm-replacement)

## Neoclouds & AI infrastructure

- `9` **AI Firm AMI to Install 9k Nvidia Vera Rubin Chips**
  AM Intelligence (Greenko founders) ordered 9,000 Nvidia Vera Rubin chips for Hyderabad and will be among Asia’s earliest adopters, targeting Q1 2027 operations; Rubin is described as up to 10x Blackwell agent throughput and 40–80% more expensive, with supplies tight versus Amazon/Google/Microsoft backlogs. The site is phase one of a 1 GW compute-as-a-service plan: 200 MW over 18 months at $8bn capex, India/Malaysia servers next year, Finland and the US in 2028, 5 GW global ambition; GPU buy is being financed with $1.5bn Japanese-bank debt plus a $1.5bn Morgan Stanley equity raise and customer-backed capital, initial capacity said (unverified) to be bought by a US customer, training first then inference. For NVDA (medium) this is a named Rubin volume and a Q1’27 go-live date on the rubin-ramp; for NBIS (full) it is a competing neocloud (neocloud-peers) using the same mix of prepay, bank debt and equity against a 200 MW near-term / 1–5 GW nameplate stack, so it is a read-through on contracted GW versus energised MW and on whether Rubin allocation is still open to non-hyperscalers. Confirm with actual Q1’27 rack-up, disclosed US offtaker and dollars-per-MW; refute if Japanese/MS financing slips or the 9k chips are delayed behind hyperscaler HBM4/CoWoS. Implies 000660 HBM4 pull if those 9k cubes ship on the stated timeline.

- `9` **Anthropic Bet Tens of Billions on AMD . One Month Later , It Signed a $45 Billion Nvidia - Powered Lease**
  Anthropic will spend $45B over six years renting Vera Rubin capacity from Nscale’s 460 MW West Virginia campus (ops late 2027), a month after committing up to 2 GW of AMD MI450 from 1H27 plus up to $5B of AMD investment into Anthropic. The $45B is a compute lease to Nscale, not a direct NVDA PO; Anthropic’s run-rate hit $65B by end-July (from $47B in May) with $190–200B 2028 revenue projected. Dual-source insurance, not AMD displacement: amd-commit, rubin-ramp, neocloud-peers (Nscale) and NVDA/AMD. Rubin racks actually energized 2H27 plus AMD 2 GW take-or-pay would confirm both books; slip, termination, or Anthropic cutting one vendor would refute. Vendor diversification also caps the NVDA pricing-power read from item 0 over time.
  Sources: [insidermonkey.com](https://www.insidermonkey.com/blog/anthropic-bet-tens-of-billions-on-amd-one-month-later-it-signed-a-45-billion-nvidia-powered-lease-1823284/)
- `9` **Cloud GPU Rental Price Index**
  AIMultiple’s 25 Aug GPU index (69 providers, 17 models) shows newest-gen on-demand (B200/B300/MI300X/RTX 5090) roughly doubled YoY as hyperscaler list prices entered the sample at 2–3x neocloud floors (B200 median $6.22, range $3.75–$16.11; B300 median $7.98; MI300X median $3.00), while H100/H200/A100 on-demand stayed in a band — H100 median ~$3.38/hr (down from >$7 in early 2024, with neocloud H100 trending down) and H200 ~$4.47 ($2.30–$13.78). One-year H100 commitments rose even as on-demand H100 was roughly flat, widening the term premium. Direct hit on gpu-rerent and the CRWV Hopper re-rent falsifier: this is not a second-hand H100/H200 collapse — neocloud Hopper is stable-to-down, not a fire sale — while Blackwell list prices are still scarcity-marked. Confirm a true re-rent/spot H100–H200 print below neocloud floors, or a CRWV/Microsoft renewal at lower $/GPU-hr.
  Sources: [aimultiple.com](https://aimultiple.com/gpu-index)

  NBIS closed ~$5.75B of converts, upsized from $4.5B then $5B: $3.45B 0.50% 2030s and $2.3B 4.50% 2034s, greenshoes fully exercised. It also exchanged $400M of 2029s and $400M of 2031s for ~15.8M Class A shares that holders may sell into the market. Stock rose 4% to $218.47, snapping a six-session slide (YTD +152%); proceeds are for DC construction and full-stack AI cloud. For the Full NBIS book this is convert + equity-via-exchange, not customer prepay or GPU-backed debt—the November Q3 print still has to show energized MW vs contracted GW and any Rubin cash rate; an ATM or another convert before that, or a dump of the 15.8M, would worsen the funding-mix read the raise was meant to settle.

- `8` **The Convertible Hangover and the Goldman Cure: Nebius's Two-Week Whiplash**
  Nebius closed a $5.75B convertible (upsized from $4.5B: $3.45B 0.50% 2030 and $2.3B 4.50% 2034 plus $750M option; $800M of older bonds swapped into 15.8M Class A), netting $4.94B for DCs; the stock then fell ~25% in six sessions before Goldman raised its PT $286→$328 (Street high) and shares bounced 5.2% to €190. Headline leverage is now ~$12B debt versus $582.3M quarterly revenue, but Wolfe says >$9B customer prepay should cover 50–60% of 2026 construction against a $37.5B backlog (Meta up to $27B, Microsoft up to $17.4B). Q2 already printed $582.3M (+454%, vs $572.75M cons), 50% adj. EBITDA margin, ARR $3.0B; FY revenue $3.0–3.4B was reaffirmed and end-2026 contracted power was raised from 4 to >5 GW, with Vineland Phase 2 approved and a Vantage Wales offtake added. For the NBIS full book this is the funding-mix print ahead of the November Q3: converts plus an equity swap, not GPU-backed debt or ATM, with prepay still the construction offset — and no disclosed Rubin cash rate or energised MW versus the new >5 GW contracted target. Confirm next print’s energised MW, prepay cash conversion, and whether convert-overhang talk fades if Microsoft-tied capacity actually commissions.

Damodaran-style TAM skepticism is already on the record; Amazon and Alphabet are both backers and Claude compute suppliers.   IREN reports FQ4 after Thursday’s close with options implying a 12.5% move; Street looks for a $0.49–0.55 loss on $130–144M revenue (down as much as 30% YoY), so the print is an AI-cloud conversion test, not a run-rate beat.   Anthropic plans to file the prospectus after Labor Day (7 Sep), hold an investor day mid-September, and list as early as late September/early October (Polymarket 86% before October), while considering both a secondary (existing holders selling in the IPO) and lock-ups longer than 180 days. Market talk is ~$1T versus banker $1.5–2T; 2028 revenue is again $190–200B, with this copy citing ~$47B annualized as of May — below the $65B end-July run-rate in the parallel item, so treat the ARR figure as disputed across sources. ## Photonics & optical

- `8` **CPO Enters Mass Production: What Material Changes Will Procurement and Supply Chain Teams Face? - WIN SOURCE BLOG**
  NVIDIA’s Spectrum-X Ethernet photonic switch entered full mass production in August 2026 and Broadcom’s 51.2T Bailly is in volume manufacturing — first real CPO production prints — with Broadcom citing up to ~70% power cut vs pluggables and NVIDIA ~5× energy efficiency and ~4× fewer lasers. Yole still has CPO at only $46m in 2024 → $8.1bn by 2030 (CAGR ~137%), so 2026 is low-volume and the volume ramp is 2027–28. New BOM concentrates InP CW lasers at Lumentum and Coherent, SiPh engines at TSMC, FAUs, plus SPIL/Foxconn/TFC, and CPO wafer-level packaging competes with AI accelerators for 2.5D/3D capacity. Touches AVGO (Bailly), LITE/COHR (InP, lite-6inch/inp-capacity) and TSM (SiPh + CoWoS crowding). Confirm 2027 switch attach and LITE/COHR InP yield/capacity; refute if 1.6T pluggable/LPO holds share and CPO stays a 2028 story.

Q1 FY27 already printed $2.418bn (+28% YoY, DC +27%); Q2 is guided $2.70bn (+35% YoY) into the 27 Aug print; stock +8% on the filing vs AVGO ~-1.6%, implying a Google dual-source read. Confirm on the 27 Aug call whether Custom Products is already on the $500m-tranche path; refute if vesting is back-end loaded or Google stays a small custom contributor in FY27.

## Semis / hardware / datacenter

- `10` **NVIDIA NVLink Fusion Brings NVHBM to Next-Generation AI Infrastructure**
  NVIDIA’s own NVHBM spec sheet: up to 30% more bandwidth per stack vs JEDEC HBM4e, up to 67% less PHY/support area (freeing up to 30% more main-die silicon), 15% lower HBM power, and a claimed 30% end-to-end XPU lift when stacked with NVLink Fusion; the power claim is quantified as headroom for up to 15,000 extra 2,000W XPUs in a 1 GW hall. NVHBM is a custom HBM base-die co-designed/validated with “leading memory vendors,” sold as the package-level complement so hyperscalers can drop custom XPUs into MGX using NVIDIA’s scale-up/out stack. This is the nvhbm-custom / 000660 question: if Rubin-era cubes ship NVIDIA’s base die rather than JEDEC HBM4e, POR share among SK hynix, Samsung and Micron becomes the mix variable, and vendor qualification is the confirm; JEDEC-only attach on custom XPUs would refute. Implies NVIDIA is pulling the memory controller into its IP even on non-GPU XPUs — a volume/pricing-power signal for whoever wins NVHBM and a share-risk versus UALink/Ethernet scale-up (MRVL).
  Sources: [news.google.com](https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMiqAFBVV95cUxQWEEwcE9ISjdxZmlzWXJiaXFEWTNmeEVCX3R0NU1Qa2ZONThvZ21DTDAwVmIyOUVJRHBLd1ZHeE5lWmROekVqTUdMQ2ExS0JpRVhwQmxSTWpFMGFTTW5yZ3g3NFRjTEc5U1AzU1Bmc01SemFlVHRzNFhOTzlRbDBnMWNpal91ZXNNT3RHYkNkVE9oZC1qOHRZcFpqbWVmbDIyMjdjMGI5MWc?oc=5) · [nvidia-dev](https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/nvidia-nvlink-fusion-brings-nvhbm-to-next-generation-ai-infrastructure/)
- `9` **Apple Mac Studio M5 Ultra and Mac Mini M6 Launched**
  Apple launched the M6 Mac mini—its first 2nm desktop SoC (12-core CPU, 12-core GPU, dual 16-core Neural Engines, 32GB unified at 170GB/s)—claiming 1.2x multithreaded CPU vs M5, ~30% more peak GPU AI compute and 10% more memory bandwidth, plus a dual Neural Engine that can run concurrently at up to 2x prior peak. The M5 Ultra Mac Studio is a quad-die UltraFusion part (>4.4TB/s inter-die), scaling to a 36-core CPU, 80-core GPU and 512GB at 1.2TB/s (+50% vs M3 Ultra) with a claimed 1.3x MT vs M3 Ultra and PCIe Gen6 SSD; the 256GB SKU is already >$11K vs ~$10K for a prior 512GB M3 Ultra, and the 512GB model is October-only. STH contrasts that with NVIDIA’s ~$100K GB300 Station at several times the memory bandwidth (e.g. >26k tok/s on Qwen3.6-35B). For coverage this is a TSMC N2 first-ship and an on-device AI SKU, not a datacenter GPU takeaway: 512GB unified memory still sits well below GB300 bandwidth, so NVDA remains the local-workstation ceiling; October 512GB ASP and whether N2 mini volume is material vs iPhone N2 timing would confirm how much wafer demand this actually adds.
  Sources: [news.google.com](https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMiZ0FVX3lxTFAtVU9NNFBqcFRMcm5fMG5ZREFqRGFDN2tTeHBkSGxfTnh2Nm9VSkRUVlV1Vkt2dWZhM1lNcTFxQUVtUWRLRXlGeklyM0M4ZGJkYUdFanZzaGhscVdORFlLMmNKZXpPSjA?oc=5) · [Vic A](https://www.servethehome.com/apple-mac-studio-m5-ultra-and-mac-mini-m6-launched/)
- `9` **OpenAI Jalapeno Custom AI ASIC at Hot Chips 2026**
  OpenAI took Hot Chips 2026 to detail Jalapeño, a Broadcom-built in-house inference ASIC+rack aimed at time-to-last-token and tokens/joule, with ~9 months RTL-to-tapeout (arch late 2024, RTL freeze 2025, late-2025 tapeout, Codex on A0 in early 2026, ChatGPT shortly after). On SemiAnalysis InferenceX (power-normalized to package TDP), Jalapeño is 700W vs GB200 1.2kW and GB300/MI355X 1.4kW and sits on the July 2026 Pareto for GPT-OSS 120B throughput/kW; OpenAI uses single-token prediction while the NVIDIA baselines use MTP (up to 8 tokens per large-model pass). GPT-OSS, DeepSeek R1 and 1T Kimi K2.5 all run despite none being co-designed for the chip. Inflection for openai-xpu/inferencex/avgo-demand: AVGO is the named shepherd, NVDA faces a public power-normalized OpenAI inference alternative, AMD is in the 1.4kW set. Confirm on InferenceX Q3 and ChatGPT production mix vs GB300/Rubin; refute if MTP-adjusted comparisons close the Pareto gap or OpenAI still scales Rubin for decode.
  Sources: [Patrick Kennedy](https://www.servethehome.com/openai-jalapeno-asic-at-hot-chips-2026/)

- `8` **Issues Stack Up With More HBM Layers**
  Hot Chips 2026 (Samsung Memory’s Sangwook Han; Objective Analysis’s Jim Handy) frames HBM stacking as hitting physical limits: die thinning to stay in package height, warpage, TSV-area vs bits, base-die PHY I/O, power density and yield—any void/misalign scraps the whole cube—so HBM consumes far more wafer capacity per bit than commodity DRAM. Handy’s shortage triad is explosive AI demand, wafers diverted into HBM, and insufficient fab capacity; Han says bandwidth has doubled each generation mainly by adding TSVs, which forces tighter TSV pitch while PHY I/O scaling is “much more complicated.” For 000660 (high book) this is the HBM4 / 16-Hi / hybrid-bonding / NVHBM-custom-base-die axis: yield and TSV/PHY bottlenecks are the mechanism for Samsung’s Rubin HBM4 share and MR-MUF vs TC-NCF, and they tighten Advantest/Teradyne test time per stack. Confirm with named HBM4 16-Hi shipments, Namics EMC, and whether custom base-die (nvhbm-custom) is how vendors escape the PHY bottleneck.
  Sources: [Ed Sperling](https://semiengineering.com/issues-stack-up-with-more-hbm-layers/)
- `8` **Explosive Semiconductor Growth in 2026**
  WSTS: global semis 2Q26 $368B, +35% q/q and +104% y/y (prior record was 1Q26 +25%/+79%). Memory QoQ: SK hynix and SanDisk +51%, Kioxia +76%; Nvidia +18% q/q; Broadcom had guided +33%. Kioxia and SanDisk are now #8/#9 after more than quadrupling y/y on AI flash. Ex-AI names in the top 20 grew only 9% q/q; 3Q guides slow (memory companies projecting <½ of 2Q rates). Full-year 2026 forecasts cluster at +90% or more (SI +101%, RCD +112%); memory +249% (WSTS) to +280% (Gartner). SI’s 2027 call is +50% vs “mid-twenty-percent” consensus, assuming slower AI plus PC/smartphone recovery as memory shortages ease. Touches SNDK (low book) and kioxia-flow / nand-cycle / LTA floors vs Goldman oversupply: flash names at 4x y/y and still guiding deceleration is consistent with take-or-pay floors ($93.9B NBM, Kioxia 2027–28 POs) remaining the swing, not a 2026 bust. A 3Q print where SanDisk/Kioxia growth halves without LTA commentary would start to validate the oversupply path.
  Sources: [Bill Jewell](https://semiwiki.com/semiconductor-services/semiconductor-intelligence/372766-explosive-semiconductor-growth-in-2026/)
- `8` **The Network Is More Of Nvidia’s Computer Than It Ever Was At Sun**
  Nvidia Q2 sales were $96.22B (+105.9% YoY). Kress said component supply supports slightly more than 70% datacenter growth in F2028; Huang said F2027 is on track to double and unconstrained F2028 “would be a lot higher”—the author infers demand ~40% above supply if a second double were possible. Purchase commitments are $279B, 2.3× the $119B at Q1-end, spanning rest-of-2027 through F2029 (tiny F2030+); the piece maps ~$365B F2027 / ~$625B F2028 GH/Vera-Rubin NVL72 toward $1T. Parallel CPU tightness: Intel Xeon shortfall “starts with a B” (~20–25% inferred), AMD Epyc over-demand perhaps ~30%, OEMs 25–30%. Touches NVDA (medium), rubin-ramp, amd-epyc and memory: F27/F28 allocation plus $279B commitments lock HBM/CoWoS demand and cap F28 DC growth near 70% unless DRAM/HBM/flash loosen. Confirm F2028 DC vs ~70% and Rubin mix; refute if commitments unwind or Huang’s unconstrained double was rhetoric.
  Sources: [nextplatform](https://www.nextplatform.com/compute/2026/08/27/the-network-is-more-of-nvidias-computer-than-it-ever-was-at-sun/5292807)
- `8` **TrendForce: DRAM, NAND Flash to Account for 68% of Major CSP CapEx in 2027**
  TrendForce projects major CSP capex +98% YoY in 2026 and +50% in 2027, with DRAM+NAND rising from 47% of that capex in 2026 to 68% in 2027. Server DRAM contracts +64% in 2H25 and ~+270% expected in 2026; eSSD +35% in 2H25 and ~+235% in 2026; even with some 2Q26+ LTA price ceilings, HBM contracts could still +70–140% in 2027. HBM+RDIMM are 51% of DRAM bits in 2026; combined server DRAM+HBM bit supply is seen +27% in 2027 from process and new fabs. Touches 000660 (high), SNDK (low), nand-cycle, sndk-lta and hyperscaler-guides: memory inflation both pads CSP capex and gives NVDA price-hike cover, while CSPs may cut HBM/RDIMM per box or hardwire ASICs. Confirm vs late-October hyperscaler prints and whether LTA ceilings bind 2027 HBM; refute if 2027 bit supply beats +27% or contracts roll over.
  Sources: [Stephen Las Marias](https://www.eetasia.com/trendforce-dram-nand-flash-to-account-for-68-of-major-csp-capex-in-2027/)

- `9` **Micron Guides to $50 Billion Revenue as AI Memory Booms - Can MU Break $1,034?** → [[Theses/MU - Micron Technology]]
  Micron posted fiscal Q3 2026 revenue of $41.46B (vs $23.86B prior quarter and $9.30B a year ago), GAAP GM 84.6%, Cloud Memory $13.77B and Core Data Center $11.52B, and guided Q4 to $50B ± $1B (≈21% sequential at the midpoint) with 86% GM and non-GAAP EPS $31.00 ± $1.00. It has begun high-volume HBM4 shipments to its lead customer, sent HBM4 qualification samples to multiple end customers, and timed HBM4E volume on 1-gamma for calendar 2027.   Lam Research broke ground Wednesday on a $620 million, 120,000-sq-ft R&D lab in Tualatin, Ore., part of $1.5 billion of Washington County lab spend over ~15 years and a >$3 billion global lab-network expansion (U.S., Asia, Europe) announced 13 Aug 2026; the building is expected to add 400 jobs to ~3,000 current headcount under a county tax-break deal in which Lam pays an estimated $111 million. - `9` **Lam Research Breaks Ground on $620M Tualatin Chip Lab** → [[Theses/LRCX - Lam Research]]
  Lam Research broke ground this week on a $620 million, 120,000-sq-ft R&D lab at its Tualatin campus, the centerpiece of a $1.5 billion ~15-year Washington County lab commitment after SIP tax incentives cleared the county (4 Aug) and city (10 Aug). The lab is expected to add ~400 jobs on a campus of ~3,000; the broader TuX plan is ~210,000 sq ft, with an economic study citing 445 permanent R&E roles at ~$173k average pay, ~$111 million in taxes/fees over the deal, and a prior $22 million Oregon CHIPS grant. For LRCX this is an R&D-capacity/network signal (lrcx-aether, wfe-forecast), not a disclosed Aether/Akara/dry-resist/400-layer tool-of-record win — it implies Lam is buying development throughput and customer proximity as GAA/etch complexity rises. - `9` **Nvidia to buy AI platform Hugging Face for $12.9 billion: Report**
  Sources disagree on status: The Information says Nvidia clinched Hugging Face at $12.9B (SiliconAngle, 27 Aug 2026 16:14 EDT), while Business Insider says talks valued HF at more than $13B, no deal is signed, and talks could still fall apart; Microsoft met but is not in ongoing talks, and Nvidia reportedly joined after Salesforce interest. Nvidia already participated in HF’s 2023 $235M round at a $4.5B valuation and last year had a $500M investment at $7B turned down; HF hosts >2 million models and is used by >13 million developers, with paid Hub and Inference Endpoints. Nvidia on Wednesday said it has $18B committed to equity investments for the rest of its fiscal year on top of $47.9B already held in private companies. For NVDA this is a software-stack/developer-lock-in bid, not a GPU-unit print; BI flags the neutrality risk (HF today hosts AMD and Intel models), so a closed deal that privileges CUDA would be the bull path and a walk-away or a neutrality covenant the refute. - `9` **🌐 Chinese chipmaker $4.9B IPO could accelerate enterprise SSD push, pressure commodity NAND prices** → [[Theses/000660 - SK Hynix]]
  YMTC’s Aug. 21 Shanghai IPO could raise ~$4.9B after Counterpoint put it at 14% of Q2 NAND shipments (tied with Kioxia; Samsung 25%, SK hynix+Solidigm 22%), 3rd in bits but only 5th in revenue. Enterprise SSDs took 48% of NAND bits vs 26% a year earlier; YMTC plans a further eSSD mix shift in H2, with 63% of proceeds for mass-production lines and 37% for R&D. Q1 chip GM was 84.23% vs 70.29% for SSDs at ~98% utilization—analysts say selling die currently out-margins finished SSDs. For SNDK/000660 this is a 2027 volume-and-mix threat once shortage eases, not a near-term HBM hit; Samsung’s July PCIe 6.0 PM1763 ramptime and whether YMTC wins server qualifications (vs remaining 5th in revenue) is the confirm/refute.
  Sources: [aninews.in](https://aninews.in/news/business/chinese-chipmakers-49b-ipo-could-accelerate-enterprise-ssd-push-while-adding-pressure-to-commodity-nand-prices20260827134248/)
- `9` **🌐 Nvidia discloses custom HBM technology 'NVHBM'** → [[Theses/000660 - SK Hynix]]
  Nvidia disclosed NVHBM (blog, Aug 26): a custom HBM stack co-developed with Amazon’s Annapurna Labs that moves the memory controller onto the HBM base die, claiming ~30% more bandwidth, lower GPU–memory power and more GPU cores in the freed XPU area. Nvidia will not fab it; it wants a “standard NVHBM implementation” across multiple memory vendors and ties it to NVLink Fusion. This is the nvhbm-custom / 000660 custom base-die axis, not JEDEC cubes—qualification and base-die IP share versus SK hynix MR-MUF/16-Hi and Samsung TC-NCF; shipping Rubin/Feynman cubes with Nvidia base-die logic would confirm, vendors staying on standard HBM4 only would refute. Also touches mrvl-fabric via NVLink Fusion.
  Sources: [edaily.co.kr](https://www.edaily.co.kr/News/Read?newsId=05018406645551584&mediaCodeNo=257)

METI subsidy plus disclosed 2027–28 purchase orders would confirm the spend is contracted; delayed capex or bits hitting commodity NAND pricing would refute. *(headline only)*

- `9` **Nvidia earnings takeaways: Huang forecasts 70% fiscal 2028 revenue growth, far above estimates** → [[Theses/AMD - Advanced Micro Devices]]
  Nvidia beat FQ2 with $96.22B revenue vs $92.17B Street and $2.22 adj EPS vs $2.10, more than doubling from $46.7B a year earlier (net income $53.95B vs $24.76B); CFO Kress said customer forecasts “point to our growth doubling next year” but put fiscal-2028 revenue growth at 70% vs 44% consensus, with the official guide still supply-constrained. New filing risk: indebtedness is now a standalone factor—$33.5B senior notes, a $25B CP program, and $15B due in 1–5 years vs $2.75B last quarter. Huang added that the AWS deal is 2 million GPUs plus “millions of CPUs” (Vera) and robotics. For NVDA (medium book) this extends duration into FY28 and is a Vera share-shift vs INTC/AMD; the debt step-up is the new cash-flow watch. Confirm the 70% on a disclosed base and Rubin mix; refute if HBM/CoWoS supply or custom silicon (AVGO/MRVL/TPU) caps the doubling.
  Sources: [www.cnbc.com](https://www.cnbc.com/2026/08/26/nvidia-nvda-earnings-report-q2-2027-live-updates.html)
- `9` **IREN's AI cloud revenue grew about 8x, but FY26 ended with a $703M loss** → [[Theses/IREN - IREN Limited]]
  IREN posted FY26 AI Cloud revenue of $128.8M (~8x from $16.4M) vs bitcoin $578.2M, signed a new multi-year deal with an unnamed frontier lab, and disclosed $4bn contracted ARR on 2026 capacity vs $1bn operating today (2026 largely sold out). Recent 3-year contracts are >$20M revenue per MW-IT (~2-year payback) with talks at ~$25M/MW; customer prepay covers 45–55% of GPU capex. Horizon 1 (50 MW IT) was delivered to Microsoft as a GB300 NVL72 Exemplar Cloud; targets are ~0.3 GW IT in 2026 and 0.8 GW in 2027. Financing: $3.6bn IG GPU debt at 6% covering 96% of the Microsoft GPU bill with prepay, plus $2.8bn non-IG (including $2.4bn Blue Owl/PIMCO at 9% for Mackenzie) funding 90% of associated GPU capex. Direct peer read-through to NBIS (full): contracted GW vs energised MW, prepay vs GPU-backed debt, and neocloud offtake/pricing. Confirm conversion of $4bn ARR into GAAP revenue and Horizon 2–4 Q4’26 delivery; refute if operating ARR stalls near $1bn.

That maps to amd-parity (Helios/MI450), amd-epyc (Venice), and amd-commit (named hyperscaler offtake), but it is secondary commentary with no GW, take-or-pay, CoWoS, or ROCm/MLPerf figures. - `9` **CoreWeave's Hidden GPU Economics**
  CoreWeave backlog reached $104.2B before another $25B+ of customer commitments in early Q3; managed-inference ARR scaled from ~$1M to >$100M with a >$250M year-end target, and A100 contracts now extend into 2029. That challenges the gpu-rerent bear that rapid obsolescence wrecks residual GPU economics and supports CRWV duration, but it is A100—not Hopper H100/H200 re-rent—and is silent on the Microsoft renewal, covenants, and dilution watches. Confirm with tenor by GPU generation and Hopper re-rent rates; a Microsoft non-renewal would still dominate the A100-to-2029 datapoint. *(headline only)*

- `9` **Nvidia's Supply Commitments More Than Doubled to $279 Billion. Here's What It Does to the Stock.**
  Nvidia’s FY27 Q2 (ended 26 Jul) revenue was a record $96.2B (>2x YoY) with data center $89.0B (+117%); supply commitments jumped from $119B last quarter to $279B, “primarily related to the procurement of memory,” ~$267B of it due by end-FY2029, and inventory rose to $31.6B from $25.8B ahead of Vera Rubin this quarter. GM was 75% (vs 72.5% LY), guided to 74% in Q3, then a 71–72% Q4 trough and 72–73% in FY2028 as price increases land in Q1—absorbing memory inflation through January then passing some through; Kress said memory prices exceeded expectations and are still rising, with 1pt of GM worth ~$1B/quarter at current scale. Q3 guide is $108.0B (~89% YoY) with zero China DC compute. This is an inflection datapoint for NVDA (medium book), 000660/HBM4 take-or-pay, MU, and rubin-ramp: confirm if HBM4/NVHBM actually ships into the Rubin intro; refute if Q4 GM breaks 71% or Rubin slips.
  Sources: [fool.com](https://www.fool.com/investing/2026/08/27/nvidia-s-supply-commitments-more-than-doubled-to-usd279-billion-here-s-what-it-does-to-the-stock/)
- `9` **TSMC's July Surge Raises the Bar for Nvidia's Foundry**
  TSMC July revenue was NT$467.58B, +44.7% YoY; first-seven-months revenue NT$2.872T, +37%. Q2 was $40.2B with 67.7% gross and 60.3% operating margin; Q3 guide is $44.6–$45.8B (midpoint $45.2B, +12.4% q/q). Shares at $415.66 sit ~30% above GF Value, so the bar for NVDA (TSMC’s largest AI customer) and tsmc-capex/nodes is that this run-rate must be filled, not just built. Transmission is foundry/packaging capacity into NVDA/AVGO/MRVL accelerators: a soft demand print from those names would hit a stock already priced for “red-hot” advanced-node and CoWoS. Next check is TSMC capex/guidance language and whether N2/A16/Arizona commentary accompanies the July surge.
  Sources: [gurufocus.com](https://www.gurufocus.com/news/9054618/tsmcs-july-surge-raises-the-bar-for-nvidias-foundry)
- `9` **SpaceX Just Announced a $100 Billion Starship Spaceport in Louisiana. Here's What It Means for Investors.**
  Starbase Louisiana is 125,000 acres with five launch complexes (two pads each), fuel, power, processing, deep-water shipping; construction 2027, first launch 2029; SpaceX cites 3,000 regional jobs. Harder book numbers: H1 2026 capex was $28.5B, “most” into SpaceXAI compute; Q2 revenue $7.8B, +92% YoY; Starlink active subscribers doubled in a year; the AI division more than tripled sales; SpaceX had 82% of commercial launches last year. Starship has still not flown a commercial mission. For high-weight SPCX this is the cleaner datapoint: AI-compute capex already dominates cash use ahead of a 2029 launch pad, so CSA length vs GPU/turbine depreciation and YE26 1.4 GW nameplate matter more than 2029 pads; Starlink “doubled subs” is not ARPU/churn after the June price rise. Wetlands/Texas lawsuits and Colossus Mississippi generator suits are the regulatory overlay.
  Sources: [fool.com](https://www.fool.com/investing/2026/08/26/spacex-just-announced-a-100-billion-starship-spaceport-in-louisiana-heres-what-it-means-for-investors/)
- `8` **🌐 SK Hynix CEO says Indiana will be key memory production base by 2030** → [[Theses/000660 - SK Hynix]]
  SK Hynix ceremonially broke ground in West Lafayette, Indiana, on its first U.S. site: a $4 billion HBM packaging plant, not a front-end fab, with the first cleanroom scheduled for October 2028. CEO Kwak Noh-Jung said Indiana will be a “key HBM production base in America” by 2030; wafers will still be made in South Korea and China and shipped in. Korea remains the capacity engine — a $720 billion buildout with production starting in February — while Commerce Secretary Lutnick had pressed SK Hynix and Samsung for U.S. front-end fabs; Kwak said the firm is “open to every site or every country.” A July Nasdaq listing raised $26.5 billion; market cap is cited near $1 trillion, up about sevenfold in a year. For the high 000660 holding this is CHIPS/onshore packaging and political optionality, not 2026–27 Rubin HBM4 wafer supply or an MR-MUF versus TC-NCF datapoint. A U.S. front-end commitment, or BIS/Lutnick conditionality, would change the read; packaging-only through 2028 leaves Korea as the bottleneck.

- `8` **Advantest Supports High-Volume Silicon Photonics Production With Integrated Test Cell < Supply Chain < 기사본문 - The Elec Inc.** → [[Theses/6857 - Advantest]]
  On 26 Aug 2026 Advantest Korea’s Park Sung-jong said Advantest has put an integrated SiPh test cell into HVM: V93000 EXA Scale ATE plus a fully integrated multi-lane HSIO module (up to 224 Gbps, no external-cable attenuation) paired with FormFactor’s Triton prober on a TEL Prexa, simultaneously measuring electrical and optical interfaces. For Low-held 6857 and coverage name FORM this is CPO/SiPh HVM-enablement (and sits next to item 1’s Nvidia light-based mass-production claim), not the HBM4 test-time-per-stack watch; confirmation is named optical-engine/CPO customers and bookings, a conference architecture slide is not.

- `8` **SpaceX will build a second, $100B ‘Starbase' spaceport in Louisiana**
  SpaceX will build a second Starbase on a former Exxon site in Vermilion Parish, Louisiana, committing $100B with construction starting 2027 and a first Starship launch in 2029 (LED is more conservative: build by end-2027, initial operations 2030); the campus is framed as a self-sustaining spaceport with propellant, power, deep-water shipping and an airport, plus 3,000 jobs. Starship is still unproven for full pad-to-pad reuse after 13 tests since 2023 (flight 14 may put the upper stage to orbit), even as Musk said Falcon will be retired once Starship flies multiple times per week; SEC filings put Starship spend above $8B, and the rocket is described as essential to Starlink growth and a planned AI-processing satellite constellation. For SPCX this is a 2027–2030 launch-capacity bet, not a change to YE26 1.4 GW nameplate, CSA life vs GPU/turbine depreciation, post-June Starlink ARPU/churn, or lock-up supply; an on-time 2029–30 first flight plus rapid reuse would confirm the Starlink/AI-sat throughput path, while a slip past 2030 or continued Falcon dependence would refute it.

- `8` **🌐 Could ChangXin Memory become China's next tech champion? Largest Chinese memory IPO launches** → [[Theses/000660 - SK Hynix]]
  YMTC filed a STAR Market IPO targeting up to 33bn yuan (record vs CXMT’s 29.5bn), with Q2 NAND shipment share cited at 14%—3rd behind Samsung 25% and SK hynix 22%—and Q1 sales 47.0bn yuan / net profit 33.4bn yuan (above CXMT’s 24.8bn), NAND ASP +173% and NAND GM 36.7%→78.7%. Implied IPO value is 275–330bn yuan; the piece itself flags NAND oversupply and price pressure from H2 next year versus DRAM/HBM support. Touches nand-cycle, SNDK (Low) and 000660: Chinese NAND bits plus IPO-funded capacity are the Goldman oversupply path against SanDisk LTA floors; listing proceeds actually adding wafers and share holding at 14% into 2027 would confirm, a delayed IPO or captive-China mix would refute.
  Sources: [newspim.com](https://www.newspim.com/news/view/20260827000398)
- `8` **🌐 Nvidia secures 386 trillion won in supply chain commitments, mainly memory procurement** → [[Theses/000660 - SK Hynix]]
  Nvidia’s Q2 FY27 disclosures show supply-chain commitments at $279B (₩386tn), up from $119B in Q1, “mainly memory procurement” (Samsung, SK hynix, Micron implied). The book is front-loaded: $92B in FY27 Q3–Q4, $87B FY28, $88B FY29, only $12B after FY30. Kress called the memory shortage a symptom of the same AI infrastructure surge driving Nvidia’s growth. Direct LTA/take-or-pay signal for 000660 (High) and SNDK/MU through FY29, not a 2030s tail; matching vendor LTA dollars and HBM4 allocation would confirm, cancellable language or a mix skew into commodity NAND into the oversupply window would refute.
  Sources: [koreatimes.com](http://www.koreatimes.com/article/20260826/1627254)
- `8` **Intuit stock plunges after weak 2027 revenue outlook: Is it a buy?** → [[Theses/INTU - Intuit]]
  Intuit guided FY27 revenue $23.28–$23.51B (9–10% vs 14% in FY26 and $23.72B consensus) and adj EPS $22.88–$23.12 vs $27.32 Street, sending the stock ~11% lower despite a Q4 beat ($4.35B vs $4.27B, +13.6%). Management is deliberately cutting TurboTax ARPC (TurboTax +2–3% vs +7% in FY26) and adding free/lite QuickBooks because “price is now the #1 reason customers leave.” This is the intu-agentic bear-watch: the print is a pricing/funnel reset, not a ChatGPT/Claude tax-filing disclosure, though the stock is already −43% YTD on disruption fears. Confirm if e-file share and payments ARPU lift LTV; refute if FY27 growth stays sub-10% and frontier-lab agents take DIY share.
  Sources: [invezz.com](https://invezz.com/en-ae/news/2026/08/26/intuit-stock-plunges-after-weak-2027-revenue-outlook-is-it-a-buy/)
- `8` **IREN Shares Drop 6% After Hours as AI Shift Spurs $639 Million Write-down** → [[Theses/IREN - IREN Limited]]
  IREN fell ~6% after hours to $38.11 as a $638.8M impairment (mostly retired miners) flipped FY26 from an $86.9M profit to a $702.6M loss; Q4 adj EBITDA dropped 68% to $19.2M as headcount nearly tripled. Q4 cash flow shows the funding mix: $2.114B investing outflows vs $5.711B financing ($3B converts + $2.112B share issuance), $1.722B deferred-revenue boost to OCF, and $7.62B cash. Management flags that contracted ARR is not GAAP and recognized sales “could be significantly lower.” For NBIS/neocloud-peers this is the dilution falsifier: 45–55% customer prepay plus GPU-backed debt still required a large convert and ATM. Confirm if equity issuance stops as Horizon ramps; refute if another convert/ATM precedes the 0.8 GW 2027 target.
  Sources: [ts2.tech](https://ts2.tech/en/iren-stock-falls-6-after-hours-as-ai-pivot-brings-639-million-impairment)
- `8` **KLA Corporation stock steadies after earnings beat as guidance and backlog support 2026 outlook** → [[Theses/KLA - KLA Corporation]]
  KLA’s fiscal Q4 revenue was a record $3.66B (+15.2% YoY) with a fifth straight non-GAAP EPS beat at $1.05; it returned $876M (~24% of quarterly revenue) and authorized a new $7B buyback. Backlog is ~$12.5B; management projects 20% 2H/1H CY26 growth and 22% CY26 revenue growth, with the September-quarter guide 1.9% above Street. This is a process-control confirmation of the 2026 WFE upcycle (KLA, kla-metrology, wfe-forecast), not a China/export-control shock. Confirm $12.5B backlog converts without HBM/advanced-packaging pushouts; refute if the 22% CY26 print is cut after hyperscaler Q3 capex.
  Sources: [www.ad-hoc-news.de](https://www.ad-hoc-news.de/boerse/news/corporate-news/kla-corporation-stock-steadies-after-earnings-beat-as-guidance-and-backlog/70003256)
- `8` **Live Nation Antitrust Lawsuit: Trump Wanted Your Tickets to Be Expensive | Balls and Strikes** → [[Theses/LYV - Live Nation Entertainment]]
  After an Oval Office meeting with Live Nation CEO Rapino in February 2026, Trump directed DOJ to settle the 2024 Biden-era antitrust case, leaving Ticketmaster a wholly owned subsidiary; Live Nation hired Sullivan & Cromwell (James McDonald, later named an SDNY deputy) and framed the suit as a Biden leftover. Breakup risk for LYV is thereby reduced and ticketing pricing power preserved—relevant only to coverage ticker LYV, not the core book. Confirm consent-decree terms if any; refute if a court rejects the settlement or states file parallel cases.
  Sources: [ballsandstrikes.org](https://ballsandstrikes.org/law-politics/live-nation-antitrust-lawsuit-trump-finger-on-the-pulse-baby)
- `8` **Meta settles social media addiction case with California, other states for $16.7 billion** → [[Theses/META - Meta]]
  Meta settled the multi-state child-safety case with a court-approved package variously cited as $16.7B (CNBC/filing), $17.1B by some AGs (includes >$459M Cambridge Analytica claims), and ~$18B by Meta itself, paid in annual installments over 10 years; Meta will accrue a previously unmodeled ~$10B legal expense in Q3 2026. California may get $1.5–2.1B, Texas settled separately for $1B, participating states take ~$12.7B (70%) outright, and $5.3B (30%) is contingent on YouTube and TikTok matching both youth-safety features (daily limits, age assurance, night mode) and cash. Product changes are due “within months”; META +~1% vs Snap −8%+ implies the tape treated this as a sector template more than a META franchise hit. For META the live item is the $10B Q3 charge versus 10-year cash; the TikTok matching clause is a tiktok-ban/divestiture transmission if ByteDance cannot implement or pay.
  Sources: [www.cnbc.com](https://www.cnbc.com/2026/08/26/meta-social-media-trial-settlement.html)
- `8` **Micron Technology Doubles Capex as AI Memory Shortage Deepens** → [[Theses/MU - Micron Technology]]
  Micron (MU) is expanding capital spending and deepening long-term customer collaborations as AI workloads lift demand for higher-capacity, higher-bandwidth memory, according to EVP Sumit Sadana; the excerpt does not quantify the “doubles capex” headline, HBM vs DRAM mix, or LTA terms, so it is only a directional MU/HBM4 demand signal until numbers appear. *(headline only)*
  Sources: [finance.yahoo.com](https://finance.yahoo.com/technology/ai/articles/micron-technology-doubles-capex-ai-210231256.html)
- `8` **GTA 6 Netflix extended look live report - all the gameplay and revelations from Rockstar's Grand Theft Auto 6 premiere | Eurogamer.net** → [[Theses/NFLX - Netflix]]
  Rockstar’s GTA 6 “extended look” ran as a Netflix timed exclusive (8pm BST, YouTube six hours later); Eurogamer’s live notes describe dual-protagonist Jason/Lucia mission play, dense NPCs, and high-fidelity systems (lean-out shooting/damage modelling, robberies, lifestyle minigames) apparently captured on PS5, with no pre-order, attach, or launch-date figures. For TTWO this is marketing cadence into the 19 November launch, not a demand print; confirmation is digital pre-order commentary or an unchanged street date, not trailer reception.
  Sources: [www.eurogamer.net](https://www.eurogamer.net/gta-6-extended-look-netflix-live-report)
- `8` **ServiceNow stock climbs after a 24% revenue jump** → [[Theses/NOW - ServiceNow]]
  ServiceNow’s 22 Jul 2026 quarter printed $3.99B revenue (+24% YoY) and $0.90 adj. EPS (vs $0.81), subscription $3.88B, and cRPO +21% to $13.20B, with FY26 subscription guidance lifted to $15.76–$15.78B (~22.5% growth). The 25 Aug close of $126.98 ($131.29B cap, well below the $194.73 52-week high; Street PT $144.24) and 26 Aug extended $122.80 make the “climbs” frame stale versus the tape. The copy has no Now Assist, seat, or ACV mix, so it neither confirms nor refutes now-assist/saas-seats; 21% cRPO plus the raise is the backlog read until the next print shows whether growth is platformizing or seat-compressing.
  Sources: [www.ad-hoc-news.de](https://www.ad-hoc-news.de/boerse/news/corporate-news/servicenow-stock-climbs-after-a-24-percent-revenue-jump/70007514)
- `8` **IREN Stock Wavers on Q4 Revenue Miss: Details**
  IREN Q4 revenue was $137.2M, 3.6% below $142.32M consensus and down from $187.3M YoY, while management cited $4B contracted ARR for 2026 capacity ($1B ARR operating today) and said 2026 capacity is largely sold out; stock was −0.27% to $40.42 in extended trading. For IREN/neocloud-peers this splits a near-term revenue miss from a sold-out 2026 offtake signal. The falsifier is conversion of that $4B ARR into energized MW and collections versus further quarterly shortfalls.
  Sources: [benzinga.com](https://www.benzinga.com/markets/earnings/26/08/61481759/iren-stock-wavers-on-q4-revenue-miss-details)
- `8` **QUICK SPARK: Jensen Huang Is '100% Confident' Frontier AI Labs Will Remain Nvidia Customers**
  On Nvidia’s earnings call, Jensen Huang said he is “100% confident” OpenAI, Anthropic and other frontier labs will remain Nvidia customers “for a very long time,” arguing their XPUs are “inference specific chips for one cloud or one service” while Nvidia is an “entire AI factory platform” spanning the lifecycle, and that those labs will still “run on NVIDIA… all around the world.” He framed equity stakes in the labs as a “once in a generation opportunity,” regretting only not investing more/sooner. That is a direct pushback on the openai-xpu / avgo-demand custom-silicon share-shift thesis for NVDA (medium book): Huang is asserting coexistence, not displacement, and labeling custom silicon as narrow inference. Confirm or refute at InferenceX Q3 and with named Anthropic TPU-gigawatt / OpenAI–Broadcom XPU offtake versus Rubin attach; the quote itself is advocacy, not a contract.
  Sources: [benzinga.com](https://www.benzinga.com/trading-ideas/movers/26/08/61470458/jensen-huang-nvidia-customers)
- `8` **Nvidia Just Did This for the First Time Ever. And These 8 Words From Jensen Huang Show Us Why It's Fantastic News for Investors.**
  Nvidia printed fiscal Q2 revenue of more than $96B, +106% YoY, vs ~$92B consensus, and EPS $2.22 vs $2.10 (+120%). For the first time it issued a full-year outlook: ~70% annual growth, with Huang saying unconstrained customer demand “could double” and that the cap is supply; he attributed the earlier guide to “a lot greater visibility now.” The piece also states Rubin production shipments are underway. For NVDA this is an inflection-style datapoint (first annual guide + supply-not-demand bind) that also feeds rubin-ramp and hyperscaler-guides: if late-October CY26 capex prints and FY27 guides do not underwrite that 70% path, the visibility claim breaks. Supply-constrained 70% vs demand-could-double implies mix/share still accrues to whoever unlocks CoWoS/HBM, not just GPU ASP.
  Sources: [fool.com](https://www.fool.com/investing/2026/08/27/nvidia-just-did-this-for-the-first-time-ever-and-these-8-words-from-jensen-huang-show-us-why-it-s-fantastic-news-for-investors/)
- `8` **SpaceX stock climbs above its IPO price: why Morgan Stanley's $300 call matters now**
  SPCX closed at $139.63, +1.2%, back above the $135 June IPO (range $104.83–$225.64) after the Louisiana Starbase plan. Morgan Stanley’s Adam Jonas reiterated a $300 PT, calling shares ~10x FY28 sales (revenue +~70%) and ~25x projected EBIT, and arguing Louisiana means Starship cadence could exceed the bank’s model (which still embeds ~$3.5T 2040 revenue without needing the site at full capacity); Wolfe kept Outperform/$175 as removing an infrastructure bottleneck. Musk-cited potential is >30 Starship launches/day; construction 2027, first launch 2029, ~$100B on 125k acres. For high-weight SPCX this is a nameplate/cadence signal, not a CSA or Starlink ARPU print: it does not address Cloud Service Agreement duration vs GPU/turbine lives, YE26 1.4 GW definition, June-price-rise churn, or lock-up supply. Falsifier is cadence/reuse slipping so Louisiana sits idle and the $300 launch-rate narrative collapses.
  Sources: [invezz.com](https://invezz.com/news/2026/08/27/spacex-stock-climbs-above-its-ipo-price-why-morgan-stanleys-300-call-matters-now/)
- `8` **SpaceX's $100 Billion Louisiana Bet Raises the Stakes for Starship**
  SpaceX plans to spend up to $100B on a Vermilion Parish, Louisiana Starship hub that CNBC/Musk-side commentary says could eventually see thousands of launches a year and ~10,000 jobs (state officials: ~3,000 direct over 10 years plus ~8,100 indirect). The article flags Texas environmental/legal overhang and that capex lands before Starship economics are proven. For SPCX this is the same cadence-unlock as the $300 call, but it raises the stake on execution and cash burn versus the book’s CSA-duration and YE26 nameplate questions; thousands of missions/year is an ambition statement, not a 2026 GW or offtake number. Confirm with permits/construction start (guided 2027) and whether launch frequency actually steps up before 2029 first flight.
  Sources: [gurufocus.com](https://www.gurufocus.com/news/9054093/spacexs-100-billion-louisiana-bet-raises-the-stakes-for-starship)
